Fast Poisson brackets and constraint algebras in canonical gravity
Will Barker

Abstract
In the study of alternative or extended theories of gravity, Dirac’s Hamiltonian constraint algorithm is invaluable for enumerating the propagating modes and gauge symmetries. For gravity, this canonical approach is frequently applied as a means for finding pathologies such as strongly coupled modes; more generally it facilitates the reconstruction of gauge symmetries and the quantization of gauge theories. For gravity, however, the algorithm can become notoriously arduous to implement. We present a simple computer algebra package for efficiently computing Poisson brackets and reconstructing constraint algebras. The tools are stress-tested against pure general relativity and modified gravity, including the order reduction of general relativity at two loops.



1
Introduction

Hamiltonian formulation of gravity

In the study of classical field theories, the canonical (phase-space) formulation is reached by foliating the spacetime into equal-time hypersurfaces, and defining canonical variables (the field components and their conjugate momenta) which evolve between hypersurfaces according to first-order Hamilton equations. In the minimal transition to the canonical formulation, it frequently happens that not all of the velocities may be solved for in terms of canonical variables. In such cases, the definitions of the canonical momenta (as the variations of the action with respect to the velocities) lead to constraints. The minimal canonical formulation can be made consistent by enforcing these constraints with the aid of extra Lagrange multipliers, and further identifying all ancillary constraints which arise by enforcing the conservation (in time, via the Hamilton equations) of the original constraints. This algorithm leads to a counting of
\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{}
pairs of Cauchy data, i.e., physical modes. The total number of constraints is partitioned into
\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{}
of the first class (commuting with all the rest), and
\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.89445pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.63889pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{2nd}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.89445pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.63889pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{2nd}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.89445pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.63889pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{2nd}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.89445pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.63889pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{2nd}}}}}{}
of the second class (non-commuting with each other). Given the original number of phase-space variables
\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{}
, the number of propagating modes is then

\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{}=\frac{1}{2}\Bigl(\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{}-2\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt and shift
\mathchoice{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
are undetermined multipliers whose values constitute a choice of gauge. For more general theories, the algorithm for determining and classifying all constraints is due to Dirac
Dirac 1950
;
Dirac 1958
and Bergmann
Bergmann and Goldberg 1955
, see also
Anderson and Bergmann 1951
;
Castellani 1982
;
Henneaux and Teitelboim 1992
;
Blagojević 2002
. This algorithm, and the formula in
eq.

1.1
, are exceptionally useful in providing a definitive counting of modes when a new classical theory is proposed. Moreover, constraints of the first class following from momentum definitions may be identified as the generators of gauge symmetries, allowing the latter to be reconstructed. In turn, this first-class algebra facilitates the quantisation of the theory
Becchi et al. 1976
;
Tyutin 1975
. Certain steps in canonical analysis would benefit from automation. These principally include the computation of Poisson brackets between arbitrary functionals of phase-space variables, and the re-expression of the results in terms of specified constraints.

In this paper

We present a set of computer algebra tools (
Hamilcar
) which facilitate canonical analysis. The
Hamilcar
package is built on top of the
xAct
suite of packages for
Mathematica

Martin-Garcia et al. 2007
;
Martín-García 2008
;
Martin-Garcia et al. 2008
;
Nutma 2014
, and automates the computation and simplification of Poisson brackets between arbitrary functionals of canonical variables in three spatial dimensions.
2
2

2

Note that
Hamilcar
is maximally theory-agnostic. It accommodates any tensorial canonical physics on a (possibly curved) three-dimensional manifold. Thus,
Hamilcar
completely deprecates the earlier software
HiGGS

Barker 2023
, which was suitable only for computing Poisson brackets in Poincaré gauge theories of gravity.

Structure of this paper

The remainder of this paper is set out as follows. In
section

2
we describe the
Hamilcar
package, and demonstrate its use by performing a full Dirac–Bergmann analysis of pure GR in
section

2.2
, and pure
\mathchoice{R}{R}{R}{R}^{2}
theory in
section

2.3
(see e.g.
Buchbinder and Lyakhovich 1987
;
Alvarez-Gaume et al. 2016
;
Hell et al. 2024
;
Golovnev 2024
;
Karananas 2025
;
Barker and Glavan 2025
). We also reconstruct the constraint algebra of pure GR at two loops, as described by the Goroff–Sagnotti action, in
section

2.4
(see e.g.
Goroff and Sagnotti 1985
;
Goroff and Sagnotti 1986
;
van de Ven 1992
;
Glavan et al. 2025
). Brief conclusions follow in
section

3
. There is one technical appendix.

Notation and conventions

We closely follow the conventions of
Barker and Glavan 2025
. We use natural units in which
\hbar=c=1
, Greek letters denote four-dimensional spacetime indices, while Roman letters denote three-dimensional spatial indices. The signature is
(-,+,+,+)
, other conventions are introduced as needed.

Installation

Acquiring the sources

The
Hamilcar
package can be installed once the
xAct
suite of packages has been installed. The
xAct
suite can be found at
xact.es
. The
Hamilcar
package is made available at the public
GitHub
repository
github.com/wevbarker/Hamilcar
, along with installation guidelines for common operating systems, including
Windows
and
macOS
. Here, we only demonstrate a
GNU/Linux
installation.
3
3

3

Note that the syntax highlighting for
Bash
differs from
Wolfram Language
highlighting used in other sections.

Bash
to download
Hamilcar
to the home directory as follows:

⬇

clone

https

//github.com/wevbarker/Hamilcar

Installing the sources

To make the installation, the sources should simply be copied alongside the other

is global, one can use:

Or, for a local installation of

Syntax highlighting

From this point, we will rely heavily on code listings in the
Wolfram Language
, the syntax highlighting for which is now described. Symbols defined in the
Wolfram Language
(
Mathematica
) are
BlackBold
, those defined in
xAct
are
NavyBlueBold
, those from
Hamilcar
are
BlueBold
, and those which are to be defined during the course of the user session are in
SkyBlueBold
. Each fresh input cell in a
Mathematica
notebook begins with the symbol ‘
In[
#
]:=

’, each output cell begins with the symbol ‘
Out[
#
]=
’. Non-parsing comments within the
Wolfram Language
appear in
(*Gray*)
, and strings (which should not be confused with symbols) are shown in
"GrayBold"
.

Note that if the global variable
$DynamicalMetric
is set to
True
, then:

•

The spatial metric
G
is automatically registered, and
ConjugateMomentumG
is defined.

•

All conjugate momenta are automatically defined as tensor densities of weight one (the effects of this are only seen by the
G

The following options may be given:

•

FieldSymbol
is the symbol that
<Fld>
will use for display formatting.

•

MomentumSymbol
is the symbol that the conjugate momentum will use for display formatting.

Function
PoissonBracket

The command

⬇

PoissonBracket
[
<Op1>
,
<Op2>
]

computes the Poisson bracket between operators
<Op1>
and
<Op2>
. The operators
<Op1>
and
<Op2>
must be valid tensor expressions involving:

•

Canonical fields and their conjugate momenta defined using
DefCanonicalField
.

•

Tensors which have been defined on the manifold
M3
using
DefTensor
, and which are assumed always to be independent of the phase-space fields unless their expansion in terms of canonical fields and fundamental independent tensors has been registered using
PrependTotalFrom
.

•

Derivatives via
CD
of canonical and non-canonical quantities, the spatial metric
G
, and the totally antisymmetric tensor
epsilonG
.

•

Constant symbols defined using
DefConstantSymbol
(or
DefNiceConstantSymbol
from the
xTras
package).

The function automatically generates smearing tensors unless
$ManualSmearing
is set to
True
, in which case
<Op1>
and
<Op2>
must be scalars (interpreted as functionals). When
$DynamicalMetric
is set to
True
, the
G
-sector contributions are included. The function computes variational derivatives with respect to all registered fields and momenta. The following options may be given:

•

Parallel
is a boolean which, when set to
True
, causes the bracket computation to be parallelised across multiple CPU cores. Default is
True
.

Function
TotalFrom

The command

⬇

TotalFrom
[
<Expr>
]

expands
<Expr>
by applying all registered rules in the global list
$FromRulesTotal
, which is populated by
PrependTotalFrom
. The function converts specially registered composite tensors into expressions involving only canonical fields and conjugate momenta defined using
DefCanonicalField
(including the pre-defined
G
and its dependencies
epsilonG
and
CD
), fundamental tensors (defined using
DefTensor
without
PrependTotalFrom
), and constant symbols defined using
DefConstantSymbol
. This command is of standalone utility, and is also used internally by
PoissonBracket
and
FindAlgebra
.

Function
TotalTo

The command

⬇

TotalTo
[
<Expr>
]

attempts to invert the expansion performed by
TotalFrom
on
<Expr>
by applying all registered rules in the global variable
$ToRulesTotal
, which is populated by
PrependTotalTo
. This function is occasionally expedient, but unlike
TotalFrom
it is mostly provided for completeness. For re-expression of functional-valued quantities, the
FindAlgebra
function is preferred.

Function
PrependTotalFrom

The command

⬇

PrependTotalFrom
[
<Rle>
]

registers the expansion rule
<Rle>
for future use by
TotalFrom
. The function adds
<Rle>
to the front of the global list
$FromRulesTotal
. It is preferred that
<Rle>
be defined by
MakeRule
, which is the safe method for defining tensor-valued replacement rules in
xAct
in such a way that all possible index valences and traces are automatically taken into account. Typically,
<Rle>
will expand a single tensor into a (possibly multi-term) tensor-valued expression. Note that
<Rle>
does
not
need to expand the single tensor in terms of canonical variables, fundamental tensors, and constant symbols. Rather, the full list of registered rules in
$FromRulesTotal
should work together to achieve this effect. This allows the sequential building up of progressively more complicated composite tensors.

Function
PrependTotalTo

The command

⬇

PrependTotalTo
[
<Rle>
]

registers the expansion rule
<Rle>
for future use by
TotalTo
. The function adds
<Rle>
to the front of the global list
$ToRulesTotal
. The considerations for defining
<Rle>
are similar to those give in
PrependTotalFrom
.

Function
FindAlgebra

The command

⬇

FindAlgebra
[
<Expr>
,{{
<Fctr1>
,
<Fctr2>
,...},...}]

seeks to express the scalar
<Expr>
(interpreted as a functional) as a sum of any number of terms, where each term corresponds to one of the sub-lists and has factors corresponding to indexed tensors whose heads are
<Fctr1>
,
<Fctr2>
, etc., where those tensor heads were defined using
DefCanonicalField
or
DefTensor
. The re-expression is achieved automatically by means of any required number of integrations by parts. The command

⬇

FindAlgebra
[
<Expr>
,{{
<Fctr1>
,
<Fctr2>
,...,{
CD
,...,
<Fctr3>
,...}},...}]

additionally admits terms where one or more spatial covariant derivatives
CD
acts on any of a select group of factors, here
<Fctr3>
, etc. The following options may be given:

•

Constraints
is a list of special (and appropriately indexed) tensors which were passed as part of the ansatz, with respect to which the re-expression is expected to be homogeneously linear. The answer will be expressed with these tensors factored out.

•

Verify
is a boolean which, when set to
True
, causes the re-expression and
<Expr>
to be varied internally with respect to any tensors which appear exactly to the first power in all terms, after an application of
TotalFrom
. This usually includes smearing functions, but it may happen to include some canonical variables. The variations are compared to ensure that the re-expression is correct. Default is
False
.

•

DDIs
is a boolean which, when set to
True
, causes all relevant dimensionally dependent identities (DDIs) such as the Cayley–Hamilton theorem to be taken into account when performing the re-expression. Default is
False
.

•

Method
allows either
Solve
(default) or
LinearSolve
.

2.2
Case study: pure GR

Motivation

The first theory we consider is Einstein’s GR in the absence of both matter and a cosmological constant, defined by the Einstein–Hilbert action

\mathscr{S}{}[\mathchoice{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}]=\int\!\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,\sqrt{-\mathchoice{g}{g}{g}{g}}\,\frac{\mathchoice{R}{R}{R}{R}}{\kappa^{2}}\,,

(2.1)

where
\kappa
is the coupling constant,
\mathchoice{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}
is the spacetime metric, and
\mathchoice{R}{R}{R}{R}\equiv\mathchoice{g^{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{{\mu\nu}}}}\mathchoice{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}
is the Ricci scalar, following from the Riemannian curvature tensor by
\mathchoice{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}\equiv\mathchoice{R^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.02058pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.02058pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.90637pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.64738pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\rho\nu}}}}{R^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.02058pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.02058pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.90637pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.64738pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\rho\nu}}}}{R^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.02058pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.02058pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.90637pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.64738pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\rho\nu}}}}{R^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[13.02058pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.02058pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.90637pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.64738pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\rho\nu}}}}
(see
appendix

A
for further index conventions). General coordinate invariance requires the use of
\mathchoice{g}{g}{g}{g}\equiv{\rm det}(\mathchoice{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}})
. Note that
eq.

2.1
is not taken to be physical until both matter and the cosmological constant are introduced; however its formal structure is well known, and it provides a useful initial test case.

2.2.1
Phase-space formulation

Lapse, shift and induced metric

The Hamiltonian treatment is to be performed using the Arnowitt–Deser–Misner (ADM) prescription according to

\mathchoice{g^{{{00}}}}{g^{{{00}}}}{g^{{{00}}}}{g^{{{00}}}}=-\frac{1}{N{}^{2}}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{g_{{{0i}}}}{g_{{{0i}}}}{g_{{{0i}}}}{g_{{{0i}}}}=\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{g_{{{ij}}}}{g_{{{ij}}}}{g_{{{ij}}}}{g_{{{ij}}}}=\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}\,.

(2.2)

The quantities in
eq.

2.2
are the lapse and shift (respectively
N{}
and
\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}
), and the residual metric on the foliations
\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}
. We introduce
\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}
as the induced covariant derivative, which satisfies the metricity condition for
\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}
. The three-dimensional curvature
\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.70319pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.70319pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.56201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jkl}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.70319pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.70319pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.56201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jkl}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.70319pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.70319pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.56201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jkl}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.70319pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.70319pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.56201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jkl}}}}
is associated with
\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}
, and should not be confused with the four-dimensional curvature
\mathchoice{R^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu\sigma\rho}}}}{R^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu\sigma\rho}}}}{R^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu\sigma\rho}}}}{R^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu\sigma\rho}}}}
. The three-dimensional curvature scalar is defined by
\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}\equiv\mathchoice{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}
, where
\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}\equiv\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.96301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.96301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.69165pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.77974pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ikj}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.96301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.96301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.69165pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.77974pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ikj}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.96301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.96301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.69165pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.77974pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ikj}}}}{\mathcal{R}^{{{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.96301pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.96301pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.69165pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.77974pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ikj}}}}
.
4
4

4

Apart from the change from Greek to Roman indices, our conventions for the curvature defined in three and four dimensions are identical.
From
eq.

2.2
we may also deduce

(2.5)

The quantity in
eq.

2.5
will be useful for the purpose of dealing with higher-order theories in
section

2.3
, and we denote the trace
\mathchoice{F}{F}{F}{F}\equiv\mathchoice{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{F_{{{ij}}}}{F_{{{ij}}}}{F_{{{ij}}}}{F_{{{ij}}}}
. The four-dimensional curvature scalar is found to be

\mathchoice{R}{R}{R}{R}\equiv 2\mathchoice{F}{F}{F}{F}+\mathchoice{K}{K}{K}{K}^{2}-\mathchoice{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}\mathchoice{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}+\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}\,,

(2.6)

where
eq.

2.6
results from some standard manipulations (see
appendix

A
). We also define another trace
\mathchoice{K}{K}{K}{K}\equiv\mathchoice{K^{{{i}}}_{{{i}}}}{K^{{{i}}}_{{{i}}}}{K^{{{i}}}_{{{i}}}}{K^{{{i}}}_{{{i}}}}
.

Hiding the velocities

By substituting the expansion in
eq.

2.6
into
eq.

2.1
we obtain

\mathscr{S}{}[N{},\mathchoice{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}},\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}]=\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}}\int\!\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,N{}\sqrt{\mathchoice{h}{h}{h}{h}}\,\Bigl(2\mathchoice{F}{F}{F}{F}+\mathchoice{K}{K}{K}{K}^{2}-\mathchoice{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}\mathchoice{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}+\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}\Bigr)\,.

(2.7)

By examining
eq.

2.5
we see that the action in
eq.

2.7
makes explicit reference to the velocity of the extrinsic curvature,
\mathchoice{\dot{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\dot{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\dot{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\dot{K}_{{{ij}}}}
and hence the acceleration of the spatial metric
\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}
, which is not desirable for the canonical approach. This term is, however, amenable to integration by parts, using the manipulation

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}{}[N{},\mathchoice{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}},\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}]

\displaystyle\supset-\frac{2}{\kappa^{2}}\int\!\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,\sqrt{\mathchoice{h}{h}{h}{h}}\,\mathchoice{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\dot{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\dot{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\dot{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\dot{K}_{{{ij}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}}\int\!\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\mathchoice{h}{h}{h}{h}}\,\left(\mathchoice{K}{K}{K}{K}\mathchoice{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}-2\mathchoice{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}\right)\left(2\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.9543pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.9543pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.59366pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.56691pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{(i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.9543pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.9543pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.59366pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.56691pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{(i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.9543pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.9543pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.59366pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.56691pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{(i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.9543pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.9543pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.59366pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.56691pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{(i}}}}\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.83858pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.83858pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.20389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.00278pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j)}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6 (2.8)

where we use

2.4
in the second line. When

is substituted back into

, the resulting action contains only velocities, and simplifies to

\mathscr{S}{}[N{},\mathchoice{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}},\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}]=\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}}\int\!\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,N{}\sqrt{\mathchoice{h}{h}{h}{h}}\,\left(\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}-\mathchoice{K}{K}{K}{K}^{2}+\mathchoice{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}\mathchoice{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}\right)\,.

(2.9)

To progress towards the phase-space formulation, we introduce an auxiliary variable
\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}
which is forced on-shell to equal the extrinsic curvature
\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}\approx\mathchoice{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}
by means of a Lagrange multiplier
\mathchoice{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}
— we know that this multiplier is precisely the momentum conjugate to
\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}
, but this is an identification that we will allow to arise naturally. The action is then

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}{}\bigl[N{},\mathchoice{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}},\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}},\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}},\mathchoice{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}\bigr]=\int\!\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,\biggl[

\displaystyle\frac{N{}\sqrt{\mathchoice{h}{h}{h}{h}}}{\kappa^{2}}\,\left(\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}-\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{K}}^{2}+\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}^{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{ij}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}\right)

Phase-space action

It is moreover possible in

for
\mathchoice{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}

2.2.2
Dirac–Bergmann algorithm

Total Hamiltonian

The variation of
eq.

2.12
with respect to
N{}
and
\mathchoice{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
reveals the constrained nature of the super-Hamiltonian and super-momentum

\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}\approx 0\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}\approx 0\,,

(2.14)

meanwhile the usual Legendre transformation is apparent in the simple format of

2.12
, leading directly to the total Hamiltonian

\mathchoice{\mathscr{H}}{\mathscr{H}}{\mathscr{H}}{\mathscr{H}}=\int\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\,\left(N{}\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}+\mathchoice{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}\right)\,.

(2.15)

Evidently,
eq.

2.15
is a functional obtained by integrating canonical variables over equal-time slices. The Dirac–Bergmann algorithm is concerned with the maintenance of the constraints in
eq.

2.14
under time evolution generated by
\mathchoice{\mathscr{H}}{\mathscr{H}}{\mathscr{H}}{\mathscr{H}}{}
. This time evolution is expressed in terms of Poisson brackets between the constraints and the total Hamiltonian. What follows is seen also in
sections

2.3
and
2.4
, and is the standard scenario in canonical gravity theories: since
\mathchoice{\mathscr{H}}{\mathscr{H}}{\mathscr{H}}{\mathscr{H}}{}
is itself expressed entirely in terms of the constraints, the Dirac–Bergmann algorithm reduces to computing Poisson brackets among the constraints themselves.

Smearing functions

Since Poisson brackets are the central currency, we introduce smearing functions to make them easier to handle. As an example of how smeared quantities are to be denoted, we follow
Barker and Glavan 2025
by writing

\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}[\mathchoice{f}{f}{f}{f}]\equiv\int\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\,\mathchoice{f}{f}{f}{f}(x)\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}(x)\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[\mathchoice{f_{{{i}}}}{f_{{{i}}}}{f_{{{i}}}}{f_{{{i}}}}]\equiv\int\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\,\mathchoice{f_{{{i}}}}{f_{{{i}}}}{f_{{{i}}}}{f_{{{i}}}}(x)\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(x)\,.

(2.16)

Whenever smearing functions appear inside Poisson brackets, they are taken to not depend on the phase-space variables
in their presented index configuration
. This means, for example, that when
\mathchoice{f^{{{i}}}}{f^{{{i}}}}{f^{{{i}}}}{f^{{{i}}}}
and
\mathchoice{f_{{{i}}}}{f_{{{i}}}}{f_{{{i}}}}{f_{{{i}}}}
appear inside two different brackets, we cannot simply conclude
\mathchoice{f^{{{i}}}}{f^{{{i}}}}{f^{{{i}}}}{f^{{{i}}}}=\mathchoice{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{f_{{{j}}}}{f_{{{j}}}}{f_{{{j}}}}{f_{{{j}}}}
. The ‘
[\dots]
’ notation of
eq.

2.16
is also used more loosely outside of Poisson brackets to denote smearing by functions which may or may not depend on the canonical variables; for clarity, constraints are factored out of such brackets as far as possible. For the super-Hamiltonian, we define a scalar smearing function:

Super-Hamiltonian auto-commutator

We can now compute the auto-commutator of the super-Hamiltonian constraint. We begin by setting up the smeared expression using two independent scalar smearing functions:

We then compute the Poisson bracket by feeding
In[
2.2.2
]
into

Out[19]=

The result of
In[
2.2.2
]
is, by default, expressed in terms of the canonical variables and their spatial derivatives, as seen in the output
Out[
2.2.2
]
. However, the goal of reconstructing the constraint algebra requires us to express this bracket in terms of the constraints themselves. The
FindAlgebra
function from
Hamilcar
performs this reconstruction automatically:

Out[20]=

We see that the
FindAlgebra
function requires us to specify in its second argument a schematic ansatz (with indices suppressed) for the final form of the bracket. In practice, such ansätze are usually easy to guess on dimensional grounds, although the specific index configurations can be tedious to enumerate in detail. In this case, we know that the final answer should be proportional to
\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
, and each of the smearing functions
\mathchoice{f}{f}{f}{f}
and
\mathchoice{s}{s}{s}{s}
, with one factor of
\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}
acting on either smearing function to provide the necessary spatial derivative. The additional structuring of the ansatz by means of nested lists signals that the
\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}
should be confined to act on the smearing functions only, and not on the constraint itself. The option
Constraints
allows the user to specify the constraints that should be factored out of the final result, which is remarkably helpful for formatting. The output
Out[
2.2.2
]
reflects

\bigl\{\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}[\mathchoice{f}{f}{f}{f}],\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}[\mathchoice{s}{s}{s}{s}]\bigr\}=\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\bigg[\mathchoice{f}{f}{f}{f}\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}\mathchoice{s}{s}{s}{s}-\mathchoice{s}{s}{s}{s}\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1 (2.18)

and
eq.

2.18
is indeed the expected result.

(2.21)

Both computations in
Out[
2.2.2
]
and
Out[
2.2.2
]
yield the same fundamental result, as seen also in
eqs.

2.20
and
2.21
: the super-momentum auto-commutator is proportional to the super-momentum itself. The apparent difference is due to the point mentioned above, that the smearing functions are taken not to depend on the phase-space variables, including the spatial metric. At the level of
xAct
, this is a feature already of how
DefTensor
works: the defined index configuration is recorded as a property of the tensor. Thus, the choice of covariant versus contravariant smearing functions leads to an implicit extra factor of the spatial metric in the arguments of
PoissonBracket
, and interactions with the momentum conjugate to the spatial metric then lead to the different final forms.

Final summary

Taken together, the brackets in
eqs.

2.18
,
2.19
and
2.20
constitute the Dirac algebra of the constraints in GR. Since each bracket is proportional to the original constraints, it follows that the constraints are all first class. It is not possible, therefore, for further constraints to arise in the Dirac–Bergmann algorithm. Taken together, the independent constraints of the first class contained in
\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}
and
\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}
are seen to be
\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{}\!=\!4
, meanwhile the independent constraints of the second class are
\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.89445pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.63889pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{2nd}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.89445pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.63889pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{2nd}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.89445pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.63889pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{2nd}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.89445pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.63889pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{2nd}}}}}{}\!=\!0
. The full number of phase-space variables distributed over the independent components of the spatial metric
\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}
and the conjugate momentum
\mathchoice{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}
is
\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{}\!=\!12
, the final number of propagating modes is found by referring back to
eq.

1.1
to be

\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{}=\frac{1}{2}\Bigl(12-2\!\times\!4\Bigr)=2\,,

(2.22)

which are accounted for by the two polarizations of the massless graviton.

2.3
Case study: pure
R^{2}
theory

Motivation

In the space of quadratic gravity theories, the pure
\mathchoice{R}{R}{R}{R}^{2}
theory is somewhat complementary to
eq.

2.1
, in that it contains no Einstein–Hilbert term. As shown in
Barker and Glavan 2025
, this feature prohibits a perturbative treatment on flat spacetime, but the canonical analysis can still be performed non-perturbatively. The action for the pure
\mathchoice{R}{R}{R}{R}^{2}
theory is

\mathscr{S}{}[\mathchoice{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}]=\int\!\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,\sqrt{-\mathchoice{g}{g}{g}{g}}\,\mathchoice{R}{R}{R}{R}^{2}\,.

(2.23)

Unlike for the case of
eq.

2.1
, there is no dimensionful coupling, and so we avoid introducing any coupling whatever in
eq.

2.23
. Note that the theory in
eq.

2.23
has — apart from its pedagogical value as an exercise in field theory — attracted attention in recent literature
Alvarez-Gaume et al. 2016
;
Hell et al. 2024
;
Golovnev 2024
;
Karananas 2025
;
Barker and Glavan 2025
, including in the Palatini formulation
Glavan et al. 2024
;
Karananas 2024
. The fully non-linear Hamiltonian analysis of the theory was presented for the first time in
Barker and Glavan 2025
; in this section we use
Hamilcar
to reproduce the results of
Barker and Glavan 2025
in somewhat more detail.

2.3.1
Phase-space formulation

Hiding the accelerations

Once again, the result in
eq.

2.6
allows
eq.

2.23
to be broken down into a more suitable notation, as

\mathscr{S}{}[N{},\mathchoice{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}},\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}]=\int\!\mathrm{d}^{4\!}x\,N\sqrt{h}\,\Bigl[2F+K^{2}-K^{ij}K_{ij}+\mathcal{R}\Bigr]\Bigl[2F+K^{2}-K^{kl}K_{kl}+\mathcal{R}\Bigr]\,.

(2.24)

The theory in
eq.

2.24
contains both velocities and accelerations, and the trick in
eq.

2.8
cannot be used to change this character. In order to make progress towards the phase-space formulation, we again introduce auxiliary fields: this time we need
\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}\approx\mathchoice{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}{K_{{{ij}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\mathcal{F}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{F}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{F}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{F}_{{{ij}}}}\approx\mathchoice{F_{{{ij}}}}{F_{{{ij}}}}{F_{{{ij}}}}{F_{{{ij}}}}
, with the fixed relation to the definitions in
eqs.

2.4
and
2.5
ensured by Lagrange multipliers. This procedure was already used in moving from
eq.

2.9
to
eq.

2.10
, but this time the introduction of the additional variable
\mathchoice{\mathcal{F}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{F}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{F}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{F}_{{{ij}}}}
requires an extra Lagrange multiplier that we call
\mathchoice{\rho^{{{ij}}}}{\rho^{{{ij}}}}{\rho^{{{ij}}}}{\rho^{{{ij}}}}
. Thus, with reference to
eqs.

2.4
and
2.5
we write

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}{}\bigl[N{},\mathchoice{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}},\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}},\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}},\mathchoice{\mathcal{F}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{F}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{F}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{F}_{{{ij}}}},\mathchoice{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}},\mathchoice{\rho^{{{ij}}}}{\rho^{{{ij}}}}{\rho^{{{ij}}}}{\rho^{{{ij}}}}\bigr]=\int\!\mathrm{d}^{4\!}x\,\biggl[

\displaystyle N{}\sqrt{\mathchoice{h}{h}{h}{h}}\,\Bigl(2\mathchoice{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{F}}+\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{K}}^{2}-\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}^{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{ij}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}+\mathchoice{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathcal{R}}\Bigr)^{\!2}

which ensures its trace-free property at all points on the foliation. To avoid such a dependence, which renders the evaluation of time derivatives tedious, it is convenient to introduce an alternative full-rank Lagrange multiplier field
