(2.29b)

Evidently, the constraints in
eqs.

2.29a
and
2.29b
differ from those in
eq.

2.13
. They can be further refined by eliminating any dependence on
\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}
, which is constrained, and this is equivalent to redefining the Lagrange multiplier
\mathchoice{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}
. Thus, a more compact (and physically equivalent) form for
eqs.

2.29a
and
2.29b
is

2.3.2
Dirac–Bergmann algorithm

Total Hamiltonian

By taking variations of
eq.

2.28
with respect to
N{}
,
\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}
, and
\mathchoice{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}
, three sets of constraints emerge, to be compared with the two sets in
eq.

2.14
for GR, namely

\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}\approx 0\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\approx 0\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}\approx 0\,.

(2.31)

By analogy with

, the Legendre transformation is now apparent in the simple format of

2.28

\mathchoice{\mathscr{H}}{\mathscr{H}}{\mathscr{H}}{\mathscr{H}}{}=\int\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\,\Bigl[N{}\bigl(\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}+\mathchoice{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}\bigr)+\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Bigr]\,,

\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}[\mathchoice{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}]\equiv\int\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\,\mathchoice{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}(x)\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}(x)\,.

(2.39)

In principle, the velocities in
eqs.

2.36
to
2.39
can themselves be used to compute all future Poisson brackets. This is sometimes done, but it offers no computational advantage: in
Hamilcar
it is the Poisson bracket operation itself that is taken as fundamental.

Primary constraint algebra

When computing the primary constraint algebra it will be useful to define the quantity

\mathchoice{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}\equiv\mathchoice{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}-\frac{\mathchoice{\pi}{\pi}{\pi}{\pi}}{3}\mathchoice{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}+\frac{\mathchoice{\rho}{\rho}{\rho}{\rho}}{6}\Bigl(\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}^{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}^{{{ij}}}}-\frac{\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{K}}{\mathcal{K}}}{3}\mathchoice{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Bigr)\,,

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Big[\mathchoice{s^{{{j}}}}{s^{{{j}}}}{s^{{{j}}}}{s^{{{j}}}}\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}\mathchoice{f_{{{j}}}}{f_{{{j}}}}{f_{{{j}}}}{f_{{{j}}}}-\mathchoice{f^{{{j}}}}{f^{{{j}}}}{f^{{{j}}}}{f^{{{j}}}}\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}} Secondary constraint algebra

As promised, the results in

,

,

,

,

and
2.46
reveal that

must also be constrained, i.e.,

\mathchoice{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}\approx 0\,,

(2.47)

since
eq.

2.47
alone forces the full algebra to weakly disappear. Let us now compute the Poisson bracket of the secondary constraint with the primary constraint:

Now we feed

2.3.2
]
into

and simplify on the primary constraint shell using

]

Out[67]=

From
Out[
2.3.2
]
the bracket between the two new constraints is

\bigl\{\mathchoice{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}[\mathchoice{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}],\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{kl}}}}{\Phi^{{{kl}}}}{\Phi^{{{kl}}}}{\Phi^{{{kl}}}}[\mathchoice{s_{{{kl}}}}{s_{{{kl}}}}{s_{{{kl}}}}{s_{{{kl}}}}]\bigr\}=\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}\bigg[\frac{\mathchoice{s}{s}{s}{s}}{3}\mathchoice{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}\bigg]+\mathchoice{\rho}{\rho}{\rho}{\rho}\bigg[-\frac{1}{6}\Bigl(\mathchoice{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}\mathchoice{s^{{{ij}}}}{s^{{{ij}}}}{s^{{{ij}}}}{s^{{{ij}}}}-\frac{\mathchoice{f}{f}{f}{f}\mathchoice{s}{s}{s}{s}}{3}\Bigr)\bigg]\,.

(2.48)

An important feature of
eq.

2.48
, which is discussed in
Barker and Glavan 2025
, is that it disappears for phase-space configurations where
\mathchoice{\rho}{\rho}{\rho}{\rho}=0
. In the bulk of the phase space, the bracket does not disappear, and so the two new constraints are generally taken to be second class. The auto-commutator of the secondary constraint self-evidently vanishes due to the definition in
eq.

2.27
, i.e.,

\bigl\{\mathchoice{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}[\mathchoice{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}{f_{{{ij}}}}],\mathchoice{\Psi^{{{kl}}}}{\Psi^{{{kl}}}}{\Psi^{{{kl}}}}{\Psi^{{{kl}}}}[\mathchoice{s_{{{kl}}}}{s_{{{kl}}}}{s_{{{kl}}}}{s_{{{kl}}}}]\bigr\}=0\,.

(2.49)

From this point onwards for the
R^{2}
model, we take the functionality of
Hamilcar
to be well-demonstrated, and we proceed directly by stating results.

Determination of the multiplier

Because of the second class property following from
eq.

2.48
, the vanishing of the velocity of
\mathchoice{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}
is not expected to lead to any more constraints. Instead, the conservation law is satisfied so long as the trace-free part of the Lagrange multiplier
\mathchoice{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}
obeys the following solution:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}-\frac{\mathchoice{\lambda}{\lambda}{\lambda}{\lambda}}{3}\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}\approx\Big(\mathchoice{\delta^{{{k}}}_{{{(i}}}}{\delta^{{{k}}}_{{{(i}}}}{\delta^{{{k}}}_{{{(i}}}}{\delta^{{{k}}}_{{{(i}}}}\mathchoice{\delta^{{{l}}}_{{{j)}}}}{\delta^{{{l}}}_{{{j)}}}}{\delta^{{{l}}}_{{{j)}}}}{\delta^{{{l}}}_{{{j)}}}}-\frac{1}{3}\mathchoice{h^{{{kl}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.98962pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.98962pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26367pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04547pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{kl}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.98962pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.98962pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26367pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04547pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{kl}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.98962pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.98962pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26367pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04547pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{kl}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.98962pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.98962pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.26367pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.04547pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makeb (2.50)

Once again, we note that
eq.

2.50
has a relevant feature, which is a direct consequence of
eq.

2.48
: a
negative
power of
\mathchoice{\rho}{\rho}{\rho}{\rho}
appears in the final term. As discussed in
Barker and Glavan 2025
, this suggests that the model may have some curious properties in the vicinity of
\mathchoice{\rho}{\rho}{\rho}{\rho}=0
. We did not encounter any solutions such as
eq.

2.50
in the case of GR in
section

2.2
. In that case, the multipliers were restricted to
N{}
and
\mathchoice{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
, which remained entirely undetermined due to the diffeomorphism invariance of the theory. The theory
eq.

2.23
is also diffeomorphism-invariant, and so no further solutions are forthcoming.

Dressing of the constraints

In order for
N{}
and
\mathchoice{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
to remain undetermined, it is sufficient, but not necessary, that the
\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}
be first class. This would, however, give rise to an inconsistency, since the dynamical evolution of
\mathchoice{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}{\lambda_{{{ij}}}}
in
eq.

2.50
is non-trivial. At an even more prosaic level, commutators of first class functions are necessarily first class, whilst
eqs.

2.41
,
2.42
and
2.43
are strongly linear in
\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}
, which are currently thought to be second class. Accordingly, we define candidate first class constraints
\mathchoice{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}
by dressing the
\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}
. The dressed constraints are defined as follows:

eq.

2.51b
simply restores the portion of
eq.

2.29b
that was lost in the shift to
eq.

2.30b
, revealing a false economy in eliminating
\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}
from
\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
. A similar effect is seen in
eqs.

2.51a
,
2.29a
and
2.30a
, though in this case the dressed
\mathchoice{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}
contains extra structures. In particular, the negative power of
\mathchoice{\rho}{\rho}{\rho}{\rho}
appears once again, as it did in
eq.

2.50
. The dressed constraints in
eqs.

2.51a
and
2.51b
have the following commutators with the secondary constraint:

(2.52a)

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\bigg[\mathchoice{f^{{{j}}}_{{{i}}}}{f^{{{j}}}_{{{i}}}}{f^{{{j}}}_{{{i}}}}{f^{{{j}}}_{{{i}}}}\mathchoice{s_{{{j}}}}{s_{{{j}}}}{s_{{{j}}}}{s_{{{j}}}}-\frac{\mathchoice{f}{f}{f}{f}}{3}\mathchoice{s_{{{i}}}}{s_{{{i}}}}{s_{{{i}}}}{s_{{{i}}}}\bigg]+\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}\bigg[\Big(\mathchoice{f^{{{k}}}_{{{l}}}}{f^{{{k}}}_{{{l}}}}{f^{{{k}}}_{{{l}}}}{f^{{{k}}}_{{{l}}}}-\frac{\mathchoice{f}{f}{f}{f}}{3}\mathchoice{\delta^{{{k}}}_{{{l}}}}{\delta^{{{k}}}_{{{l}}}}{\delta^{{{k}}}_{{{l}}}}{\delta^{{{k}}}_{{{l}}}}\Big)\mathchoice{s^{{{l}}}}{s^{{{l}}}}{s^{{{l}}}}{s^{{{l}}}}\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{k}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{k}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.932 (2.52b)

As required, the commutators with
\mathchoice{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}
in
eqs.

2.52a
and
2.52b
vanish weakly. Moreover, the dressing corrections in
eqs.

2.51a
and
2.51b
are all linear in
\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}
, so that
\mathchoice{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}
and
\mathchoice{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
preserve the property of
\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathcal{H}}
and
\mathchoice{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathcal{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
— as seen in
eqs.

2.44
and
2.45
— of still weakly commuting with
\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}
. Taken together, these observations support the hope that
\mathchoice{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}
and
\mathchoice{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
may indeed be first class.

The deformed Dirac algebra

This property is confirmed by the strong algebroid

\displaystyle\bigl\{\mathchoice{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}[\mathchoice{f}{f}{f}{f}],\mathchoice{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}[\mathchoice{s}{s}{s}{s}]\bigr\}

which vanishes weakly. At the strong level, although the commutators in

Final summary

To collect our results together, we find that the full counting of independent components among the first class
\mathchoice{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}{\mathfrak{H}}
and
\mathchoice{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{H}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
is
\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.27922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.28831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.49167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{1st}}}}}{}\!=\!4
. The full counting of second class components among
\mathchoice{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}{\Phi^{{{ij}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}{\Psi^{{{ij}}}}
is
\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.89445pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.63889pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{2nd}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.89445pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.63889pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{2nd}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.89445pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.63889pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{2nd}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.8195pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.89445pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.63889pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{2nd}}}}}{}\!=\!10
, where we recall that each of these tensorial constraints is trace-free and symmetric. Compared to the case of GR, we have
twice
as many phase-space variables distributed between the spatial metric
\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}
and the conjugate momentum
\mathchoice{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}
, and also the auxiliary extrinsic curvature
\mathchoice{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\mathcal{K}_{{{ij}}}}
and its conjugate momentum
\mathchoice{\rho^{{{ij}}}}{\rho^{{{ij}}}}{\rho^{{{ij}}}}{\rho^{{{ij}}}}
— a full counting of
\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.11118pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.71112pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.22221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Can}}}}}{}\!=\!24
. The final number of propagating modes is found from
eq.

1.1
to be

\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.77783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.53195pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09027pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\text{Phy}}}}}{}=\frac{1}{2}\Bigl(24-2\!\times\!4-10\Bigr)=3\,.

(2.55)

These are accounted for by the two tensorial polarizations of the graviton, plus an additional scalar mode arising from the higher-derivative nature of the theory.

2.4
Case study: pure GR at two loops

Figure 2:
Essential diagrams contributing to the two-loop divergence of pure GR in the background field approach, once the internal lines are carefully treated
Goroff and Sagnotti 1985
.

Motivation

The final gravity theory we consider is an extension of
eq.

2.1
with a cosmological constant and a cubic Riemann correction term, defined by the action

\mathscr{S}{}[\mathchoice{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g_{{{\mu\nu}}}}]=\int\!\mathrm{d}^{4}x\,\sqrt{-\mathchoice{g}{g}{g}{g}}\,\biggl[\frac{\mathchoice{R}{R}{R}{R}-2\Lambda}{\kappa^{2}}+\alpha\kappa^{2}\mathchoice{R^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\sigma}}}}{R^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\sigma}}}}{R^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\sigma}}}}{R^{{{\mu\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\sigma}}}}\mathchoice{R^{{{\rho\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\di (2.56)

where
\Lambda
is the cosmological constant and
\alpha
is a small coupling parameter. The specific cubic Riemann term considered in
eq.

2.56
arises as the leading quantum correction at two-loop order in perturbative quantum gravity
Goroff and Sagnotti 1985
;
Goroff and Sagnotti 1986
;
van de Ven 1992
. Working from
eq.

2.1
, the Gauss–Bonnet identity and field equation
\mathchoice{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}\approx 0
together eliminate all possible one-loop divergences in the
S
matrix, and the correlators may be rendered finite by a field reparameterisation
’t Hooft and Veltman 1974
. At two loops, however, there are insufficiently many such identities. It was shown in
Goroff and Sagnotti 1985
;
Goroff and Sagnotti 1986
that the divergent part of the effective action at two loops may be computed via six contributions in the three-point correlator, as illustrated in
fig.

2
. These divergences do not cancel, and cannot be removed by field redefinitions: upon further computation they can be seen to necessitate the cubic Riemann counterterm in
eq.

2.56
. This result was confirmed in
van de Ven 1992
. We did not include
\Lambda
in
eq.

2.1
, and without detailed calculation it might be expected that such an addition will lead to fewer cancellations and more curvature counterterms, even at the one-loop level
Christensen and Duff 1980
;
Goroff and Sagnotti 1986
;
Bastianelli et al. 2023
. In fact, the only result is that
\Lambda
itself is renormalised
Christensen and Duff 1980
;
Percacci 2017
.

Need for order reduction

As a truncated effective field theory, the action in
eq.

2.56
is largely unsuitable for direct phenomenological study. This aspect has perhaps been addressed most thoroughly in recent work
Glavan et al. 2025
, though it comes four decades after the original formulation by Goroff and Sagnotti. Being higher-order in derivatives,
eq.

2.56
is expected to introduce additional degrees of freedom which (with or without
\Lambda
) do not smoothly connect to the particle spectrum of
eq.

2.1
. A general-purpose order-reduction algorithm — acting directly on the phase-space action — was laid out by Glavan in
Glavan 2018
, based on earlier work in
Jaen et al. 1986
;
Eliezer and Woodard 1989
. When applied to
eq.

2.56
in
Glavan et al. 2025
, this method was found to require unusually lengthy manipulations. We take this
tour de force
as a reasonable upper bound on the complexity of canonical operations in gravity, which therefore provides a natural opportunity to stress-test the
PoissonBracket
and
FindAlgebra
functionalities of
Hamilcar
. In
Hamilcar
, the cosmological constant is defined as:

and the Wilson coefficient
\alpha
as:

The gravitational coupling
\kappa
was already defined in
section

2.2
via
In[
2.2.1
]
, so we do not redefine it here.

2.4.1

Reduced phase-space action

The
reduced
phase-space action was found in
Glavan et al. 2025
. The procedures used to obtain it can be implemented in
Hamilcar
, but are not instructive for the purposes of this paper, so we simply state the final result as

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}\bigl[N,\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}},\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}},\mathchoice{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}\bigr]=\int\!d^{4\!}x\,\left(\mathchoice{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}{\pi^{{{ij}}}}\dot{\mathchoice{h}{h}{h}{h}}_{ij}-N\mathchoice{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{H}}-\mathchoice{N^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\m It is an essential point that
eq.

2.58
has precisely the same structure as the phase-space action of GR given in

and

2.28
has a richer symplectic structure and different constraints: the idea is that the higher derivative order in this latter theory is faithfully reflected in the canonical analysis, whereas the two-loop cubic Riemann theory in
eq.

2.56
must pass through a process of order reduction to produce
eq.

2.58
. The corresponding

Expansion in the Wilson coefficient

To facilitate the constraint algebra analysis, we decompose the reduced Hamiltonian constraint into zeroth-order and first-order parts in the Wilson coefficient
\alpha
, denoted as
\mathchoice{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{H}}=\mathchoice{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}+\mathchoice{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(1)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(1)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(1)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(1)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}

The only interesting part of the constraint algebra is the auto-commutator of the reduced super-Hamiltonian. To analyze it, we compute the Poisson bracket as a sum of three contributions, to first order in the Wilson coefficient
\alpha
, namely

\left\{\mathchoice{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{H}}\left[\mathchoice{f}{f}{f}{f}\right],\mathchoice{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{H}}{\mathbb{H}}\left[\mathchoice{s}{s}{s}{s}\right]\right\}=\left\{\mathchoice{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}\left[\mathchoice{f}{f}{f}{f}\right],\mathchoice{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}\left[\mathchoice{s}{s}{s}{s}\right]\right\}+\left\{\mathchoice{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}\left[\mathchoice{f}{f}{f}{f}\right],\mathchoice{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt} Zeroth-order in
\Lambda

The most complicated part of the total bracket is the contribution at zeroth-order in the cosmological constant
\Lambda
, which contains both zeroth and first-order terms in the Wilson coefficient
\alpha
. We extract this contribution using the utility function defined above:

The raw extracted bracket from
In[
2.4.2
]
is extremely lengthy and difficult to interpret directly; we do not display its output here. To make progress, we apply the
FindAlgebra
function from
Hamilcar
iteratively, each time specifying a different ansatz structure that we want the bracket to match.
9
9

9

It is not necessary that we do this, and we show all the possible configurations to indicate the power and flexibility of
FindAlgebra
. One could skip ahead to the most compact form given in
Out[
2.4.2
]
.
The
FindAlgebra
function attempts to re-express the bracket through a combination of integration by parts (modifying boundary terms) and applications of Cayley–Hamilton-like identities in three dimensions. These algebraic manipulations are performed automatically; the user need only specify the desired structural form. We proceed through four progressively refined representations. First, we express the bracket in terms of first-order gradients of the shorthand tensorial momentum
\mathchoice{\mathscr{K}^{{{i}}}_{{{j}}}}{\mathscr{K}^{{{i}}}_{{{j}}}}{\mathscr{K}^{{{i}}}_{{{j}}}}{\mathscr{K}^{{{i}}}_{{{j}}}}
:

\mathchoice{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}
. Since
\mathchoice{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}{\vphantom{l^{l^{2}}}\smash{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\scalebox{0.55}{$(0)$}}}}{{\scalebox{0.99}{$\mathbb{H}$}}}}}
itself contains a term linear in
\Lambda
, and we are working at zeroth-order in
\Lambda
, we must wrap this computation in a
Block
command that temporarily sets
\Lambda=0
. This prevents
FindAlgebra
from attempting to expand terms that cannot appear at this order. Additionally, we use the
Constraints
option to instruct
FindAlgebra
to factor the result in terms of these constraint functions:

Out[94]=

The result
Out[
2.4.2
]
reveals the structure of the constraint algebra at zeroth-order in
\Lambda
and facilitates direct comparison with
Glavan et al. 2025
.

First-order in
\Lambda

The raw extracted bracket from

and

(2.64)

It is easy to confirm using
eqs.

2.59a
,
2.59b
,
2.62a
and
2.62b
that our computed results match
eq.

2.64
perfectly, up to additional DDIs that may themselves be resolved by
FindAlgebra
. The deformed algebra results from the difference between the constraints in
eqs.

2.59a
and
2.59b
and those of pure GR in
eq.

2.13
. It was shown in
Glavan et al. 2025
that this difference is an essential property of the order-reduction of
eq.

2.56
, resulting in a
minimally modified
theory of gravity
Lin and Mukohyama 2017
;
Mukohyama and Noui 2019
;
Aoki et al. 2019
. This concludes our discussion of the
Hamilcar
suite of tools.

3
Conclusions

In this paper we have presented
Hamilcar
, a
Wolfram Language
package for computing and simplifying Poisson brackets in canonical field theory. The package was shown to be effective against highly non-trivial operations in the canonical formulation of gravity. This includes the automated reconstruction of closed-form constraint algebroids through integration-by-parts manipulations, and dimensionally-dependent identities such as the Cayley–Hamilton theorem. Many of the techniques used by
Hamilcar
have been available for some years, though an effective implementation was lacking. It is expected that
Hamilcar
will be useful for the order-reduction (via the algorithm in
Jaen et al. 1986
;
Eliezer and Woodard 1989
;
Glavan 2018
) of those modified gravity theories, and theories beyond the standard model of particle physics, which present as truncated effective theories. The expected outputs in such cases are quantum-corrected Hamiltonia which are suitable for phenomenological study. Current limitations of the package include the lack of support for fermionic fields, and for anonymous scalar-valued functions of scalars. The latter in particular are already part of the
xAct
ecosystem, and could be integrated into
Hamilcar
in future work.

Appendix A
Gauss–Codazzi and the Ricci scalar

In this appendix, we provide a more conventional introduction to the ADM formalism
Arnowitt et al. 2008
than that given in
section

2.2.1
, allowing for a self-contained derivation of the formulae of
eqs.

2.5
and
2.6
which lie at the heart of our approach — we do not rely on computer manipulation
Peeters 2007a
;
Peeters 2007b
;
Glavan et al. 2025
. To begin with, our conventions for the curvature are as follows. Given some vector

(A.2)

It is a defining characteristic of the phase-space formulation that a definite sense of time is agreed upon in the calculations; this we parameterise with the coordinate
t\equiv\mathchoice{x^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
. The remaining coordinates
\mathchoice{x^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{x^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
, with holonomic Roman indices running from one to three, are taken to be ‘
adapted
’ in the sense that they span each spatial hypersurface for each respective value of
t
. Having set up the coordinate system, the conventional starting point for the ADM formalism is to introduce the unit-timelike vector field
\mathchoice{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\equiv-1
with the property of being normal to spatial hypersurfaces

\mathchoice{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{K}{K}{K}{K}\equiv\left(\mathchoice{\dot{K}}{\dot{K}}{\dot{K}}{\dot{K}}-\mathchoice{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{N^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$ . The explicit time derivative of the whole trace requires some further manipulation: since
\mathchoice{\dot{K}}{\dot{K}}{\dot{K}}{\dot{K}}\equiv\mathchoice{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\dot{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\dot{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\dot{K}_{{{ij}}}}{\dot{K}_{{{ij}}}}-\mathchoice{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}{K^{{{ij}}}}\mathchoice{\dot{h}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{\dot{h}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{\dot{h}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{\dot{h}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}
we can use

A.4
to write

\mathchoice{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{K}{K}{K}{K}\equiv\frac{1}{N}\left(\mathchoice{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{{ij}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\script
